# Package adoption register

**Status:** Preliminary for Stage 1, decided on 2026-10-21 under interim authority ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), amended on 2026-10-22 (changes C01 to C11) ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)); on 2026-10-27 only its statements of the governing issue-format version and of the round step that applies answers were updated ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), and three times on 2026-10-28 only the statement of the governing issue-format version ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated); [ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated); [ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), with section 4.4 listing the points left open; Stage 2 dispositions proposed ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)). Written in phase P1 of the integration plan (2026-10-19; phases in section 4) and completed in phase P7 (2026-10-21). Where this document differs from the provenance ledger, the decided sections of [Architecture](../ARCHITECTURE.md) and [Open questions](../OPEN_QUESTIONS.md), or [Issue format](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md), those texts take precedence (order in section 3).\
**Purpose:** This register records the proposed handling of every part of an external, AI-generated design package. It is the only place where package identifiers, such as `role.verifier`, `G-READY` or `REQ-06`, get a disposition. Other ARIADNE documents use ARIADNE names and link here.\
**Stage:** Stage 1 and 2 ([stage legend](#41-stage-legend)). Sections 1 to 5, 7 and 10 to 12 apply to both stages; sections 8 and 9 list package content that is Not adopted (in section 8, only the version-pattern part); each row of sections 6 and 12.1 carries its own label.\
**Attribution:** Adapted from design package ARIADNE-AGENT-ARCH-001 v1.2.1 (AI-generated for Tillman Heisner, 2026-10-12).

This describes a proposed system, not implemented software.

## 1. What the package is

- **Name:** ARIADNE agent architecture design package, ID ARIADNE-AGENT-ARCH-001, design patch 1.2.1 (contract 1.2), dated 12 October 2026.
- **Origin:** generated by an external AI design run for Tillman Heisner, starting from ARIADNE commit a2b3c77. Not published.
- **Contents:** 363 files: a main architecture document, 14 specification files (13 machine-readable, one Markdown), 104 example files (103 synthetic JSON fixtures and their manifest), diagrams, a design charter, review and validation records, and supporting evidence.
- **Identity:** zip archive SHA-256 `e0b070124fc39d611b8439f8d30bb0e4c2e7d368593c0e826a0bf27a04673f40`. Tillman Heisner holds the original. It is not part of this repository.
- **Standing in ARIADNE:** its Stage 1 parts are adopted as the preliminary Stage 1 design of ARIADNE by the documented Decision [Staged Stage 1 agent design](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated), and its Stage 2 parts are proposed by the documented Proposal [Stage 2 agent design](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated) (decision D4). Before those entries this register only proposed a handling for each part. The package's own "approved" decisions (DEC-01 to DEC-11) are not ARIADNE decisions.

## 2. Attribution and licences

- Text adapted from the package carries the attribution line shown at the top of this document.
- Tillman Heisner commissioned the package and releases the adapted text under ARIADNE's documentation licence, [CC BY 4.0](../LICENSE).
- Nothing third-party is copied: no Hermes Agent code (MIT licence, Nous Research), no arXiv text and no package prompt text. Upstream sources are cited and linked: EnzymeML at commit [bf9321f](https://github.com/EnzymeML/enzymeml-specifications/blob/bf9321f720eaff48d315bad52aebc1765cf42188/schemes/v2/enzymeml-v2.json), Hermes Agent at the tag chosen under D5, and papers by arXiv id (R03 and R06 at version 1).
- The package's scripts are never run, and its files are not committed.

## 3. Precedence and pins

**Precedence**, highest first:
1. the provenance ledger in [README](../README.md#provenance-ledger);
2. the decided sections of [Architecture](../ARCHITECTURE.md) (Status, Proposed flow, Components, Principles) and of [Open questions](../OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) (Q1-Q4);
3. [Issue format](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md);
4. the decisions of Tillman Heisner listed in section 4.3, documented in the Stage 1 design (D4) and which rank below Issue format;
5. the design documents in this folder;
6. the package, only through this register.

Where Issue format states a more detailed rule on a topic that Architecture covers in summary (for example the citation check, Issue format 8.2), the Issue format rule applies.

**Pins**
- **ARIADNE:** the consolidated documentation commit; execution records must identify the exact commit in use.
- **Hermes Agent:** tag v2026.9.24, commit f97608f178d1ffeca59860195ab7da295f7c8e5f, released 2026-09-24, chosen by the rule of decision D5 (newest stable tag at least two weeks old); facts re-checked at this tag in [Runtime](RUNTIME.md). The package's pin, commit 82ef40ab, is an untagged main-branch commit and is not adopted.
- **EnzymeML:** V2.0 at enzymeml-specifications commit bf9321f ([Open questions, Q3](../OPEN_QUESTIONS.md#q3---which-enzymeml-target-should-be-tested)), validated under [Issue format, section 9](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#9-validation-profile). Schema SHA-256 `1d001e351ac2c0191241f6152cbd4cd4f61fbdb196cdae0672531d209365a227`, checked against upstream on 2026-10-12 and 2026-10-19.

## 4. Labels, decisions and phases

### 4.1 Stage legend

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Stage 1 | Needed for pilot Stage 1 (15-25 public STRENDA entries, one approving scientist and no separate curator role). Written in full. |
| Stage 2 | Needed later. Written in full, as compact tables, in the target document. Items that Issue format section 11 lists as "Stage 2, if ever" are proposals for Stage 2, not commitments. |
| Stage 1 and 2 | A light version in Stage 1, the full version in Stage 2. |
| Not adopted | Superseded by an ARIADNE rule, or left out. The register gives the reason. |
| pending decision (Dn) | Waits on an open decision listed in 4.4 (not used at present). |

Moving a Stage 2 item into Stage 1 needs a new Decision entry in the ledger.

### 4.2 Treatment legend

| Treatment | Meaning |
|---|---|
| adopt | Taken over in substance. |
| adapt | Taken over with ARIADNE changes. |
| superseded | Replaced by an ARIADNE rule, named in the row. |
| stage-2 | Deferred in full to Stage 2. |
| left-out | Not taken over; the row gives the reason. |

### 4.3 Decided by Tillman Heisner on 2026-10-19, not yet in the ledger

These decisions guide this register and remain documented here. The runtime choice dates to 2026-10-19; Stage 1 design decisions and their dates are listed below. The section heading is kept so existing links stay valid. Changes C01 to C11, decided on 2026-10-22, are listed last; they amended Issue format from version 0.5 to 0.6. The current preliminary version is 0.10. The [consolidated ledger record](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated) records documentation consolidation, not a new scientific decision or a reversal of an earlier main entry.

- **D1:** The adopted design lives in a `design` folder of eight documents; the decided sections of Architecture stay unchanged.
- **D2:** The package is kept outside the repository, is never unpacked inside it, and is credited by the attribution line.
- **D3:** One commit per phase on branch `dev`, each after review and approval; design documents stay marked Draft until phase P7.
- **Runtime:** Hermes Agent is the Stage 1 runtime, pinned to a tagged release, with one fresh process per agent attempt and only ARIADNE's tools; its containment is proven in Stage 1 by an automated test suite. The runtime choice is documented in [Runtime](RUNTIME.md); the ledger records documentation consolidation.
- **Token caps and limits:** Per-request token caps are disabled during development; caps and other limits are set later from measured needs.
- **D4:** Option A: the runtime choice (phase P2), version 0.5 (D7), the preliminary Stage 1 design and the proposed Stage 2 design (phase P7), documented in this register; later Results for the containment suite and the measurements, and the Decision that freezes the evaluation caps (phase P8).
- **D5:** Option A: the newest stable Hermes Agent tag that is at least two weeks old, with v0.21.7 as fallback. On 2026-10-19 this gave tag v2026.9.24 ([Runtime, section 3](RUNTIME.md#3-pin)).
- **D9:** Option A: ARIADNE specifies the evaluation modes (each agent alone; in sequence through round 1 only), the input-set and example manifests, packets, run records and separation rules, and proposes the expected-side formats, planted-defect families and canary strings, which Tillman Heisner accepts, changes or replaces; he freezes each interface version ([Evaluation interfaces](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md)).
- **D6:** Option A (2026-10-20): Stage 1 agents get no tools; the whole input and needed reference text go in the packet; read tools are specified for Stage 2.
- **D8:** Option A (2026-10-20): derived JSON Schema files accompany the formats in design/specs, headed "derived from <document> <version>; the document wins".
- **D13:** Option A (2026-10-20): software-computed checksums in the run log only; the record format stays unchanged.
- **D14:** Option A (2026-10-20): at most one technical re-run of an agent after unparsable, truncated or crashed output, carrying only the error; counted and reported.
- **D7:** Option A (2026-10-20): one bundled Issue format version 0.5 with the items Tillman Heisner accepted; documented in section 11 and the [Issue format change list](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#changes-from-version-04).
- **D10:** Option A (2026-10-20): one container per agent attempt (Docker or Podman); its only network access is a local model gateway that holds the provider key, logs every physical request including retries, and refuses fallback and helper calls.
- **D11:** Option A1 (2026-10-20): one model route for all three agents, Anthropic Messages protocol family; the dated model is chosen in phase P8; no fallback models; never the ChatGPT Codex backend.
- **D12:** Option A (2026-10-20): in development no token caps (max_tokens is the route's documented maximum, passed explicitly); delegation, fallback, helper calls and agent tools stay at zero as containment rules; provisional runaway guards on attempts per stage and wall-clock per attempt, marked not calibrated; Hermes retries at 0 where configurable, others counted at the gateway; rounds counted, not capped. In evaluation, caps at about twice the measured maximum, recorded by a ledger Decision and frozen before any test-set run; a cap hit is a technical failure.
- **D15:** Option A (2026-10-20): after the agent is built, the runner removes every tool from Hermes's registry, as defence in depth; the start-up self-check confirms an empty registry, and the direct-dispatch tests also run with removal off ([Runtime, section 14.1](RUNTIME.md#141-options-and-recommendations)).
- **D16:** Option A (2026-10-20): every Stage 1 attempt runs with HERMES_SAFE_MODE=1, so that no general plugin, shell hook or outbound webhook loads; the planted-plugin tests run with the variable set and unset.
- **D17:** Option B (2026-10-20): all three agents, the inquiry agent included, use Hermes's agent loop with an empty tool list; the single structured call is not used.
- **D18:** Option A (2026-10-20): a stop at the wall-clock guard, a container-limit hit and a cut stream are class crashed and get the one technical re-run of D14.
- **D19:** Option A (2026-10-20): provisional values, not calibrated: 60 minutes per attempt, 12 attempts per pipeline stage of one round, max_iterations 4, and an active request guard of 10 requests per attempt.
- **D20:** Option A (2026-10-20): container limits on CPU, memory and processes, with values set from the phase P8 measurements and marked not calibrated until then.
- **D21:** Option A (2026-10-20): the test-only gateway settings and launcher variants of [Containment](CONTAINMENT.md), accepted only while the gateway's upstream is the stub model.
- **D22:** Option A (2026-10-20): the run-time monitors for connections, processes and file events also run in real runs; an unexplained record discards the output and suspends the run.
- **D23:** Option A (2026-10-20): in Stage 1 a question-screen hit is recorded and counted only; the round is not held for the curator (curator role removed on 2026-10-22, C10; the round is not held for human review either).
- **D24:** Option A (2026-10-20): a ledger Result entry for the first VERIFIED containment report at each Hermes pin, and for any VERIFIED report that follows a FAILED one.
- **D25:** Option A (2026-10-21): the proposed reading of D14 in Handoffs 2.2 and 2.13 and Workflow 4.4.2 is confirmed: output that is not JSON or fails its format check is unparsable; a turn without a normal text exit is crashed; the limit applies per agent step; an attempt of which no request was forwarded, or that the gateway made void, uses up no re-run. Phase P7 removed the 'to be confirmed' wording there.
- **D26:** Option A (2026-10-21): under D6 A the inquiry agent's packet has no INPUT block; its questions rest on the issues, their judgments and their quotes ([Handoffs, section 2.4](HANDOFFS.md#24-input-packets)).
- **D27:** Option B (2026-10-21): after an unexplained monitor record (containment_hit) in a real run, every run under the same runtime manifest is blocked until the containment suite passes again ([Containment, section 12.4](CONTAINMENT.md#124-the-admission-check)).
- **D28:** Option A (2026-10-21): D25 A covers every void attempt (a refusal by the model gateway, a start-up self-check that fails after the turn, and a containment_hit); none of them uses up the technical re-run.
- **D29:** Option A (2026-10-21): the derived schema files of the remaining handoff formats are written in phase P8, with the implementation.
- **D30:** Option A (2026-10-21): two points that Issue format leaves open (whether a reopened issue keeps closed_by, and the value of not_available in an answer item) are carried to its next version.
- **D31:** Option A (2026-10-21): the checksum of an approved record covers the evidence file with its approval entry, taken after that entry is written (D13 A).
- **D32:** Option A (2026-10-21): a containment report follows a FAILED one when the most recent earlier report at the same Hermes pin, capture-mode baseline runs not counted, was FAILED (D24 A).
- **D33:** Option A (2026-10-21): in mode B of the evaluation interfaces, unwanted questions are counted against the expected verdicts, each listed against the step that let its issue reach the inquiry agent, never against the inquiry agent ([Evaluation interfaces, section 2.2](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md#22-mode-b---in-sequence-through-round-1)).
- **D34:** Option A (2026-10-21): the rule that a parent never shares a packet with its variant covers only an item's own input and frozen files; examples stay under leave-one-out ([Evaluation interfaces, section 3.5](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md#35-packets-for-evaluation)).
- **D35:** Option A (2026-10-21): the readings of the planted-defect families DV1 and DV11 in [Evaluation interfaces, section 5.1](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md#51-planted-defects) are confirmed: a null or empty-string placeholder at a field with a role gives both wrong_type and the field's missing issue, as does an empty object at a unit field; an empty list or whitespace-only text gives only the missing issue. Issue format stays unchanged.
- **D7 item 1:** Accepted: a schema "required" error at an empty field that has a field role is raised only as a missing_* issue; part of Issue format since version 0.5 (section 11).
- **C01:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Live pointers are bound to the ids of the objects they address, retargeted only where the correspondence is one-to-one and checked before use; an ambiguous target holds the issue, evidence entry or parameter link until a named person maps or disposes it ([Issue format 7.1](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#71-flow), "Live pointers").
- **C02:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Every structural edit records its basis: source locators that clearly state the change, an explicit human statement, or both; an explanation alone is never source evidence, and an ambiguous source needs a statement ([Issue format 6.2](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#62-structural-edit)).
- **C03:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Example manifests carry the item's actual fold (dev-T9HQ4X, dev-GNICYO or test) in both conditions; the fold `all` is removed, and a zero-shot manifest has no examples ([Evaluation interfaces, section 3.3](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md#33-example-manifest)).
- **C04:** Decided on 2026-10-22: In mode A the frozen data blocks are identical across conditions and repetitions; in mode B the original input and the fixed configuration are identical, and downstream blocks are each run's own outputs ([Evaluation interfaces, section 3.5](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md#35-packets-for-evaluation)).
- **C05:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Expected judgments and issues are keyed per mode: in mode A by frozen issue id, kind and pointers, in mode B by source anchor, field and kind; results that cannot be mapped go to the review list and are never scored silently ([Evaluation interfaces, section 4](EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md#4-expected-side-proposed)).
- **C06:** Decided on 2026-10-22: The request class `unclassifiable` is allowed only for refused requests with a refusal reason; such a request is never forwarded and counts as refused and against the request guard ([Run records, section 2.5](RUN_RECORDS.md#25-the-request-log)).
- **C07:** Decided on 2026-10-22: A reported partial usage count is kept and flagged as incomplete in `usage_unknown`; flags carry into totals, and incomplete counts never calibrate caps ([Run records, section 4.4](RUN_RECORDS.md#44-unknown-usage)).
- **C08:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Outcome `technical_failure` requires a failure class, except in the two documented classless request-limit cases (`runaway_guard`, `cap_hit`) ([Run records, section 2.4](RUN_RECORDS.md#24-attempt-entries)).
- **C09:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Identifier and checksum patterns match the whole string only, rejecting a final newline, surrounding whitespace and overlength under both ECMA-262 and Python `re`; values are never trimmed ([Derived schema files](specs/README.md#how-to-check-a-file)).
- **C10:** Decided on 2026-10-22: There is no separate curator role: a named person working on the record, normally the scientist, answers, takes the human actions and, if eligible, approves without switching roles; issues formerly routed to the curator go to human review; assisted entry keeps the author and confers no approval authority ([Issue format 6](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#6-human-actions)).
- **C11:** Decided on 2026-10-22: Shared model-facing material (role prompt, reference text, output-format text) holds no worked task demonstrations; demonstrations appear only in the few-shot examples block from reviewed eligible manifests ([Roles, section 7.2](ROLES.md#72-packet-layout)).

### 4.4 Open decisions

Decisions D10 to D35, which were open during phases P4 to P7, are listed in section 4.3. Tillman Heisner left three points open on 2026-10-28 after the later decisions of that day; for the third, the boundary was then decided, and its mechanism stays open. Until each is decided, the conservative behaviour that the documents describe stays in force, and nothing here authorizes an automatic conversion, a default, wider admission of data or new model semantics.

1. **Pipeline runs under provisional deadlines.** Whether provisional monitoring deadlines may admit pipeline runs that are explicitly identified as using development data. Until it is decided, no pipeline run is admitted under provisional deadlines ([Containment, section 12.4](CONTAINMENT.md#124-the-admission-check), test MH-12; [Runtime, section 10.3](RUNTIME.md#103-the-sequence), step 3).
2. **Hill exponent without a stated unit.** Whether a Hill n that the record states with no unit may receive a narrowly defined dimensionless unit from the model instead of going to human review. Until it is decided, such an n fails check 4 of [Issue format 3.1](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#31-software-fields) and goes to human review ([Handoffs, section 2.15](HANDOFFS.md#215-parameter-link-entries), test 12).
3. **The rate's unit and its later use.** The boundary is decided ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)): checks 4 and 5 of [Issue format 3.1](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#31-software-fields) establish compatibility inside the formula only; an equation is recorded and reviewed without a numerical-compatibility claim; before it is represented as suitable for a specified numerical use, such as the derivative of a species, its rate's unit and scale are checked against that use, so that a rate in µmol/L per second is never used as mmol/L per second for dS/dt; an unspecified target gets no claim, and a specified target that does not match gets neither the equation nor a conversion, and goes to review; approval is no evidence of compatibility (Issue format 3.1, "Numerical use", and 7.2). Still open: how a numerical target and its unit are determined, how an equation is integrated into a downstream tool, and any conversion for that use. Until they are decided, no target is specified, so no equation carries a numerical-compatibility claim ([Handoffs, section 2.15](HANDOFFS.md#215-parameter-link-entries), tests 10, 13 and 14).

### 4.5 Phases of the integration plan

| Phase | Work |
|---|---|
| P1 | This register and the design index in Architecture |
| P2 | Hermes Agent facts re-checked at a tagged release; first part of design/RUNTIME.md |
| P3 | Evaluation interfaces, first version |
| P4 | Roles, handoffs and workflow documents; then the pending issue-format changes (D7) |
| P5 | Runtime and containment test suite |
| P6 | Run records and budgets; evaluation interfaces, second version |
| P7 | Traceability, full review and the adoption entries in the ledger |
| P8 | Implementation, containment run and measurements, only when Tillman Heisner asks for it |

## 5. Name table

This is the only place where package role and service names are mapped to ARIADNE names.

| Package name | ARIADNE name | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `role.extractor` | extraction agent |  |
| `role.verifier` | verification agent | Not the deterministic verifier. |
| `role.inquiry` | inquiry agent |  |
| `svc.deterministic_verifier` | deterministic verifier | Reads the input only to check citations. |
| `svc.controller` | workflow controller |  |
| `svc.answer_applicator` | answer application | Applies answer items as defined in Issue format, section 5.2. |
| `svc.policy_broker` | model gateway (Stage 1); broker (Stage 2) |  |
| `svc.ledger` | run log (Stage 1); record store (Stage 2) | In ARIADNE, "ledger" means only the provenance ledger. |
| `human.scientist` | scientist | One named scientist approves. |
| `human.data_owner` | data owner | Admission of sources and model routes is Stage 2 (Q6). |
| none | named person's human actions; human review | Added by ARIADNE ([Issue format 6](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#6-human-actions)): the named person working on the record, normally the scientist, takes the human actions and handles review items. The separate curator role was removed on 2026-10-22 (C10). |
| none | assembly step | Added by ARIADNE (Issue format 7.1 step 2). |
| part of `svc.controller` | final generation step | Issue format 7.1 step 9. |

## 6. Coverage register

One row per package part or identifier: 656 rows. 626 rows are derived mechanically from the package, plus one row per package file or folder (30 rows); method in section 12.
Treatments: adapt 337, adopt 109, left-out 81, stage-2 70, superseded 59.
Status "done" means nothing is left to write: the item is covered by Issue format, Architecture, a design document in this folder or this register, or it is superseded or left out. No row is "planned": phase P7 completed every target document. A "done" row may cite an item of section 11 by its number. The rows of sections 6.7 to 6.9 are the dispositions of the package's requirements, scenarios and decisions; section 12.1 links each one to the ARIADNE text that carries it.

| `file:specs/workflow_specification.yaml` | States, guards, transitions, lifecycle, approval rules and preflight | adapt | Stage 1 and 2 | design/WORKFLOW.md 2-4, 9 | done | C-02, C-07, C-08, C-11, C-20, C-23 | Mapped to Issue format rounds in WORKFLOW prose. Issue lifecycle, candidate guards and standards preflight superseded (ISSUE_FORMAT 4, 7.1, 9); approval per ISSUE_FORMAT 7.2; replay Stage 2; idempotency keys Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |

### 6.2 Data formats

| Key | What | Treatment | Stage | Target | Status | Ref | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Identifier` | Common syntax for ids in handoffs and records | adopt | Stage 1 | design/HANDOFFS.md 2.1 | done | C-14 | Applies to run, record, attempt and artifact ids. ARIADNE ids such as ISS-001 and Q-001 fit it; software assigns them, never an agent. |
| `Digest` | Lowercase hexadecimal SHA-256 checksum string | adopt | Stage 1 | design/RUN_RECORDS.md 1.2, 2.9 | done | C-21, C-22, D13 | Always computed by software, never by agents. Stage 1 uses it for the input file (Q1); further checksums: D13 A, in the run log only (Run records 2.9). |
| `Pointer` | RFC 6901 JSON Pointer into the record document | adopt | Stage 1 | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 2 | done |  | ISSUE_FORMAT 2 already defines pointers into the document, for software only; scientists never see them (ISSUE_FORMAT 5.3). |
| `Actor` | Fixed list of agents, software services and people | adapt | Stage 1 and 2 | design/ROLES.md 2.1, 3.1, 4, 5.2 | done | C-09, C-15, C-25, C-33 | Section 5 maps the names; human actions of a named person, assembly and final generation steps added. Broker duties go to the model gateway and containment; svc.ledger becomes the run log; data-owner admission is Stage 2 (Q6). |
| `State` | List of workflow states for a record | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 2 | done | C-10, C-11, C-15, C-23 | Mapped to ARIADNE states, adding assembly and final generation. blocked_scientific becomes 'not approvable' (ISSUE_FORMAT 5.4); the verification agent runs in round 1 only; the standards denial suspends nothing (ISSUE_FORMAT 9 item 1). |
| `Context` | Run, record and revision ids carried by every handoff | adapt | Stage 1 and 2 | design/HANDOFFS.md 2.1, 7.1 | done | C-14, C-21 | Stage 1 handoffs carry run id, record id and round, written by software. Revision ids and same-record ancestor rules belong to revision chains: Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |

Binding in every handoff needs file hashes: Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |
| `AuthRef` | Broker-verified identity of the scientist or data owner | adapt | Stage 1 and 2 | design/RUN_RECORDS.md 2.1, 2.9, 3.6 | done | C-25, C-36 | Stage 1 records the named scientist (answered_by, approved_by; ISSUE_FORMAT 5.2, 7.2) and stores answer files unchanged with their origin. Broker-verified authentication is Stage 2 (Q7); data-owner identity too (Q6). |

Empty-quote absence locators superseded (C-01). Source ids, byte offsets Stage 2; file hashes Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |

Attachment sources and parser profiles for derived spans are Stage 2. |

### 6.3 Transitions, guards and guard predicates

| Key | What | Treatment | Stage | Target | Status | Ref | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T-01` | Start extraction after admission, context and budget checks | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 3.1 row 2, 3.3 | done | C-23, C-25, C-26, C-28, C-29, D4 | Starts one extraction attempt in a fresh process once the input checksum is recorded and the containment test suite has passed (documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)). No data-owner assertion, standards denial or token reservation. |
| `T-02` | Hand the extraction output on to the deterministic check | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 3.1 row 4, 3.3 | done | C-04, C-15, C-18, C-22, D7 | Passes first through the assembly step, which fills the SOFTWARE fields (ISSUE_FORMAT 7.1 step 2). The extraction output carries a draft and evidence entries, no issues, missing registry, classification or hashes; not-mapped entries: Issue format 7.1 step 1; 8.1 (this register, section 11, item 3). |
| `T-03` | Move from the deterministic check to the verification agent | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 3.1 row 9, 3.2 | done | C-10 | Stage 1 takes this step in round 1, where even a failing draft goes on to the verification agent. Later rounds send new issues straight to the inquiry agent, barring the fallback re-run on fields written by structural edits (ISSUE_FORMAT 7.1 step 8). |
| `T-04` | Move from verification to inquiry once the report is complete | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 3.1 rows 15 to 17, 3.3 | done | C-02, C-12 | The report passes its format check (Handoffs 2.8). The agent is asked to judge every issue except missing_optional (ISSUE_FORMAT 4.2 rule 1); an issue left without a judgment or with two different verdicts is content, recorded as not judged and routed like uncertain (rule 4), not a failed guard. Issues the agent adds carry no judgment and get software ids. The package's candidate-result precondition is superseded (C-02). |
| `T-05` | Send questions to the scientist and wait for answers | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 3.1 row 20, 3.2 | done | C-11, C-31 | Questions follow ISSUE_FORMAT 5.1; issues routed to human review go to the review queue instead (ISSUE_FORMAT 4.2 rule 6). Rounds are counted and reported; Issue format sets no round cap. |
| `T-06` | Make the record approval-ready when nothing is left to ask | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 3.1 rows 12, 17 and 27, 4.3 | done | C-15, C-20, C-31 | Goes through final generation and a deterministic re-check, then waits for the scientist when ISSUE_FORMAT 7.2 conditions 1-3 hold. Zero questions never means approval. |

### 6.4 Operations, containment paths and denials

| Key | What | Treatment | Stage | Target | Status | Ref | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tool.source_read` | Agent read tool for slices of the admitted input | stage-2 | Stage 2 | design/ROLES.md 8.2, 8.8 | done | D6, D26 | D6 A (decided 2026-10-20): Stage 1 agents get no tools; the extraction and verification agents get the whole input in their packet (the inquiry packet has no INPUT block: D26 A, section 4.3); the tool is specified for Stage 2. |
| `tool.reference_read` | Agent read tool for the pinned schema and unit references | stage-2 | Stage 2 | design/ROLES.md 8.2, 8.8 | done | D6, D26 | D6 A (decided 2026-10-20): Stage 1 agents get no tools; the extraction and verification agents get the whole input in their packet (the inquiry packet has no INPUT block: D26 A, section 4.3); the tool is specified for Stage 2. The deterministic verifier reads the pinned schema directly, not through this tool. |
| `tool.citation_check` | Software-only check that a cited span exists and holds the value | adapt | Stage 1 | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8.2 | done | C-17, D7 | Already the citation check C-CITE (ISSUE_FORMAT 8.2): xpath resolves, quote verbatim, value equals quote, not containment. Byte spans are Stage 2; artifact hashes Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11); 'each xpath selects exactly one node': Issue format 2, Locator; 8.2 check 1 (this register, section 11, item 2). |

Field-level output dropped: the deterministic verifier knows no input format (ISSUE_FORMAT 8, 11); byte locators Stage 2. |

| `op.coverage.check` | Deterministic verifier checks every input token is mapped or listed | superseded | Not adopted | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8, 11 | done | C-01, C-18 | Superseded by ISSUE_FORMAT 8 and 11: the deterministic verifier reads the input only for citations. The test runs with removal on and off, and both runs must pass. HERMES_SAFE_MODE=1 keeps every plugin out (D16 A); the test runs with the variable set and unset. |

| `messaging` | Denied call: sending messages | adopt | Stage 1 | design/CONTAINMENT.md 4.5, 6 | done |  | Agents contact no one; questions reach the scientist only through software, from the inquiry agent's output. Tested by containment test CT-05. |
| `arbitrary_plugin_dispatch` | Denied call: dispatching tools through any plugin | adopt | Stage 1 | design/CONTAINMENT.md 4.4, 6 | done | D16 | Plugin dispatch cannot reach a tool outside the admitted set. Tested by containment test CT-04. No plugin loads: HERMES_SAFE_MODE=1 (D16 A). |
| `tool_search` | Denied call: Hermes tool search | adopt | Stage 1 | design/CONTAINMENT.md 4.2, 4.6, 6 | done |  | Tool search could expose or load hidden tools, so calls are denied. Tested by containment test CT-02. |
| `inline_tool_bypass` | Denied call: inline tool execution around the checks | adopt | Stage 1 | design/CONTAINMENT.md 4.6, 6 | done |  | Inline tool executors must not run tools outside the admitted set or skip the checks. Tested by containment test CT-06. |
| `connection_management` | Denied call: managing tool or service connections | adopt | Stage 1 | design/CONTAINMENT.md 4.5, 4.8, 5.1, 6 | done | D5 | The package names it without defining it. At tag v2026.9.24 (D5 A) its meaning is settled: the connection-management tool and the connector route (Runtime H-V9.25; Containment CC-25 and CT-25). Covered by containment tests CT-05, CT-08 and CT-25. |

### 6.8 Conformance scenarios

| Key | What | Treatment | Stage | Target | Status | Ref | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SC-01` | Test: clean path ends with explicit approval by the scientist | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 5.1, 6.1 row 21 | done | C-20, C-23, C-31 | With zero questions the record goes to final generation and is approved only by one named scientist listed among the creators (ISSUE_FORMAT 7.1 step 9, 7.2), never automatically. The version-denial premise is superseded (ISSUE_FORMAT 9 item 1). |
| `SC-02` | Test: required name absent across several input sources | superseded | Not adopted | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4.2 | done | C-01, C-04 | Tests a multi-source coverage registry, which ISSUE_FORMAT 11 rejects. Replaced by ISSUE_FORMAT 8 and 4.2 rule 5: the deterministic verifier raises missing_required without reading the input, and the verification agent confirms it. |
| `SC-03` | Test: optional field left empty stays visible as a limitation | adapt | Stage 1 | design/WORKFLOW.md 6.1 row 11 | done | C-06 | Rule already covered by ISSUE_FORMAT 3.5 and 4.1: an empty description is a LIST field, listed as missing_optional, never asked and never blocking. There is no dismissal answer; ASK fields take not_available with a reason (5.2 rule 6). |
| `SC-04` | Test: structured and free-text values disagree about temperature | adopt | Stage 1 | design/EVALUATION_INTERFACES.md 5.1, 5.2 | done | C-04 | Rule already covered by ARCHITECTURE.md Principles (Extract, do not guess) and ISSUE_FORMAT 4.1 note 1: the structured value stays, and the verification agent raises source_conflict citing both. ISSUE_FORMAT example 10.1 shows the same rule for pH. |

It closes with a corrected unit, and the correction entry keeps the original (8.1). |

| `DEC-01` | Package default: fully specify Stage 1, other inputs only as extension points | adopt | Stage 1 | design/HANDOFFS.md 2.3 | done | C-34 | Package default, not an ARIADNE decision (C-34). Stage 1 is written in full (section 4) for STRENDA XML input (Q1); the issue format is input-independent (ISSUE_FORMAT 1, 8). The input packet names its format, so others can be added. |
| `DEC-02` | Package default: Hermes integration delegated to capability audit, thin controller allowed | adapt | Stage 1 | design/RUNTIME.md 2, 5.1, 10 | done | C-26, C-27, C-28, C-34, D4, D5 | Proposed: replaced by the documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated) (section 4.3): Hermes is the Stage 1 runtime at a tagged release (D5 A), one fresh process per attempt, containment proven by a test suite (C-26 to C-28). |
| `DEC-03` | Package default: software applies scientist patches, re-runs both checks; changes void approval | adapt | Stage 1 | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 5.2, 7.1, 7.2 | done | C-07, C-10, C-20, C-34, D7 | Software applies answer items, not patches (Issue format 5.2); only the deterministic verifier re-runs each round (7.1). A full verification-agent re-run is not in Stage 1, possibly Stage 2 (C-10). |

Model choice: one route for all three agents (D11 A1). |

Shell, Python and web stay denied, proven by tests (documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)). |

## 7. Conflict register

Each conflict between the package and ARIADNE, the ARIADNE text that governs it, and the resolution.

| # | Topic | Package says | ARIADNE rule | Governed by | Resolution |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| C-01 | Deterministic verifier reads input beyond citations (coverage, mapping) | The deterministic verifier runs a source coverage check, raises mapping_omission and missing_not_mapped, and needs all-source coverage for each missing entry, despite the package's own REQ-19. | The deterministic verifier reads the input only to resolve xpaths and quotes for the citation check and knows no input format. A missing-field registry with input search coverage, and STRENDA mapping in the deterministic verifier, are rejected. | ARCHITECTURE.md Components (Deterministic verifier); ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8 and 11 | ARIADNE wins; these checks are superseded, not deferred. The deterministic verifier runs only C-SCHEMA, C-REF, C-CITE, C-UNIT and the R-* role rules. Omitted values are found by the verification agent (missed_value) or by evaluation. |
| C-02 | Verification agent barred from adding gap issues | The agent may add only source_conflict and semantic_concern issues. Other suspicions become report-only 'mechanical candidates' that software classifies, with at most one automatic diagnostic revision (T-15) per content revision; later new findings suspend the run. | The verification agent confirms or rejects each issue and adds any it finds. It raises wrong_unit, source_conflict, wrong_value and missed_value directly; its own issues carry no judgment, and software assigns their ids. | ARCHITECTURE.md Components (Verification agent); ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4, 4.1 and 4.2 | ARIADNE wins: the mechanical-candidate protocol, its classifier and the diagnostic revision (T-15) are superseded. The package's aim survives in Issue format 4.2 rule 2; 6.2; 7.1 (this register, section 11, item 5): software registers an issue only from the raiser its kind allows. |
| C-03 | Issue kind list | Fifteen kinds, each stored with check class, requirement and blocking flag, including wrong_reference, schema_failure, missing_evidence, missing_not_mapped, mapping_omission, semantic_concern, technical_artifact_failure and metadata_configuration. | Ten kinds, including missing_recommended and missed_value; missing_optional covers LIST fields and ASK-IF fields whose rule does not apply (Issue format 4.1). Blocking is computed, not stored. Values copied faithfully but looking implausible are out of Stage 1 scope. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4 (rule 1) and 4.1 (table and note 3) | Issue format wins. Same-named kinds map directly, except package missing_optional, which becomes missing_recommended (ASK, or ASK-IF while its rule applies) or missing_optional (LIST, or ASK-IF whose rule does not apply). Also: wrong_reference to broken_reference; schema_failure to wrong_type; missing_evidence to bad_citation; mapping_omission to missed_value; semantic_concern (unsupported value) to wrong_value; metadata_configuration to a SOFTWARE-field issue routed to human review; technical_artifact_failure to a run-log record (Issue format 4.2 rule 2; 6.2; 7.1 (this register, section 11, item 5)); missing_not_mapped dropped (C-01). |
| C-04 | Extraction agent raises issues and keeps a missing registry | Extraction steps X03 and X04 emit conflict issues and a registry of every absent field; the draft bundle carries issues, and the extraction agent is a permitted issue originator. | The extraction agent extracts only. Issues are raised only by the deterministic verifier or the verification agent, which raises source conflicts. The structured value is kept and the conflict is raised. | ARCHITECTURE.md Components (Extraction agent) and Principles (Extract, do not guess); ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4 and 4.1 | ARIADNE wins. The extraction agent outputs a draft and evidence entries (plus not-mapped entries: Issue format 7.1 step 1; 8.1 (this register, section 11, item 3)); no issues, registry, classification or hashes. It keeps the structured value, and the verification agent raises the conflict. |
| C-05 | Who raises wrong_value and wrong_unit | wrong_value and wrong_unit are mechanical kinds that only the deterministic verifier may raise. | wrong_value is raised by the verification agent. wrong_unit is raised by the deterministic verifier (unit unparsable or of the wrong dimension) or by the verification agent (unit differs from the source). A value not equal to its quote is bad_citation. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4.1 and 8.2 | ARIADNE wins: the Issue format 4.1 raisers and meanings apply, and the package's list of mechanical kinds is superseded for these two kinds. |
| C-06 | Field requirement model and optional-gap dismissal | Fields are required, optional or not applicable. Scientists dismiss optional gaps with a rationale (DEC-07, CORE-ADR-04, dismiss_optional); readiness needs those dispositions, and the inquiry agent asks for them. | Fields have roles SOFTWARE, ASK-REQ, ASK, ASK-IF and LIST. ASK fields close with a value or with not available plus a reason code. Empty LIST fields are never asked and never block. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 3, 4.1 and 5.1 (rule 2) | ARIADNE wins; the Issue format 3 role table is the completeness profile. dismiss_optional maps to not_available with a reason code on ASK and ASK-IF fields (units: a value only); empty LIST fields are only listed. DEC-07 is superseded; its semantic dismissals map as in C-32. |
| C-07 | Answers as scientist-authored typed JSON patches | Answers have six kinds and carry typed patch operations with expected-before SHA-256, rationale, authentication and an idempotency key; implicit cascading edits are forbidden (CORE-ALG-02). | An answer lists items {issue_id, value, choice or not_available, unit, reason_code, note, field}. Software writes each value to its issue's fields and re-applies the SOFTWARE rules. Idempotency keys and revision chains are 'Stage 2, if ever'. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 5.2, 6.2, 7.1 (step 7) and 11 | Issue format wins; scientists never write patches, and software re-applies the SOFTWARE rules after answers. provide_value and correction become a value or choice; cannot_answer becomes not_available (units: a value only); resolve_conflict becomes a choice; dismiss_optional and dismiss_semantic map as in C-06 and C-32. Expected-before hashes, receipts and replay are Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |
| C-08 | Issue lifecycle and closure | Lifecycle states are open, resolved_by_recheck, accepted_limitation and superseded; only a deterministic re-check clears a mechanical failure. | Status is open, unavailable, held or closed; closed_by is a question id, recheck, override or structural_edit. On re-check the same kind and fields keep their id; closed issues reopen unless closed by not available, keep or override. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4, 5.4 and 7.1 | Issue format wins. resolved_by_recheck maps to closed_by recheck, and accepted_limitation to 'not available' or a keep decision; superseded is not used in Stage 1. Mechanical issues may also close by an answer or an override (C-09). |
| C-09 | No human actions; no person closes mechanical failures | Ten actors and no curator. Required absence and deterministic failures can never be dismissed or waived by anyone. | A named person working on the record, normally the scientist (in Stage 1 the scientist or Tillman Heisner), overrides mechanical issues with a reason code and makes structural edits with their basis, without switching roles. Likely checker defects and SOFTWARE-field issues go to human review as review items. There is no separate curator role (removed on 2026-10-22, C10). | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 6, 6.1, 6.2, 6.3 and 4.2 (rule 6); ARCHITECTURE.md Components (Scientist); [Workflow and review design consolidation](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated) (documentation record); [Issue format, changes from version 0.5](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#changes-from-version-05) | ARIADNE wins: human actions are added, taken by a named person working on the record, with only the duties of Issue format 6.1 (override) and 6.2 (structural edit) and the one act that 4.2 rule 6 adds (sending a rejected mechanical issue that the person reviewing finds real on to the inquiry agent); assisted entry (6.3) confers no approval authority. Overrides are recorded, shown at approval and counted in evaluation. The no-waiver rule still binds agents; people close mechanical issues only by a recorded override and never waive any other issue. |
| C-10 | Verification agent re-runs after every change | Every revision reruns the deterministic checks and a fresh independent verification (G-REVISE, T-09, CORE-ALG-03 step 2, INV-17, DEC-03, V05). | In rounds 2 and later only the deterministic verifier re-runs, and new issues go straight to the inquiry agent. The verification agent does not re-run in Stage 1, except possibly on fields written by structural edits if those edits prove error-prone. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 7.1 (step 8) | Issue format wins. A re-run on fields written by structural edits only is a Stage 1 fallback; recommended: switched on only by a ledger-recorded decision. A full re-run of the verification agent every round is not in Stage 1 and may be considered for Stage 2; INV-17 is adapted accordingly. |
| C-11 | Round and inquiry-attempt caps; blocked state | Clarification rounds are capped at four per record and T-11 blocks at the cap; inquiry attempts, repairs included, are also capped at four, so budgets may stop earlier (INT-C04). | Steps 5-8 repeat until no askable issue is open; Issue format sets no round cap. An unavailable required field still blocks, and the record ends as 'not approvable', naming those fields. Limits are set later from measurements. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 7.1 and 5.4; ARCHITECTURE.md Proposed flow ('until no questions remain'); token-cap decision of 2026-10-19 (section 4.3) and D12 A | Proposed: no round cap in Stage 1; rounds are counted, and limits are set later from measurements. The package's blocked state maps to 'not approvable', entered once no askable issue and no review item is open and one is unavailable. Attempt limits become provisional runaway guards (D12 A, D19 A). |
| C-12 | Judging missing_optional issues | The verification agent must judge every supplied checker issue (V04, G-VERIFY). | The agent judges every deterministic verifier issue except missing_optional (as Issue format 4.1 defines it, C-03), which it judges only by exception; issues it adds itself carry no judgment. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4.2 (rules 1 and 2) | ARIADNE wins: G-VERIFY's 'every issue judged' becomes 'every issue except missing_optional', and added issues need no judgment; a missing judgment no longer stops the move (ISSUE_FORMAT 4.2 rule 4). |
| C-13 | Empty values and placeholders | null is allowed where the schema permits it, source-stated empty strings and lists may be kept, and present empty or meaningless objects get their own representations. | Omit, don't null: drafts leave empty fields out, and a null, "" or {} placeholder is a wrong_type issue. Empty includes whitespace-only text, [] and a unit with neither a name nor base units; 0 and false are values. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 2 | Issue format wins. A per-object 'meaningless object' profile is a Stage 2 proposal. How a schema 'required' error at an empty field is raised: Issue format 4.1 note 4; 8 (since v0.5; this register, section 11, item 1). |
| C-14 | Object and issue ids | Stable issue ids derive from scope, rule and anchor; unreconcilable issues get a superseding issue instead of fuzzy merging; never invent a domain id. | Software writes every id field (S-ID). Issue ids are ISS-nnn, never chosen by an agent and never reused; a problem found again with the same kind and fields keeps its id. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 3.1 (S-ID), 4 and 7.1 | Issue format wins. The extraction agent uses local link names, the assembly step writes object ids, and the workflow controller assigns issue and question ids. supersedes_issue_id may return in Stage 2. |
| C-15 | Software-filled fields, origins, assembly and final generation | Software writes only /version and whitelisted normalizations. Origins exclude structural edits; there are no vessel, symbol, rate-law or date rules and no assembly step. | A separate assembly step fills S-VERSION, S-ID, S-UNIT, S-VESSEL, S-SYMBOL and S-RATELAW; the final generation step sets S-DATES before approval. filled_by is extraction_agent, software, scientist or structural_edit. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 3.1, 7.1 (steps 2, 7 and 9) and 8.1; ARCHITECTURE.md Proposed flow | ARIADNE wins: assembly and final generation are added as software steps with rule_id evidence, and package origins map to filled_by plus structural_edit. The package's final materialization before approval (CORE-ALG-03 step 1) becomes final generation. |
| C-16 | Unit conversion changes stated values | A whitelisted microlitre-to-litre operation multiplies stated values by 10^-6, keeping the original as derivation evidence (CORE-ADR-12, SC-30). | The extraction agent copies what the input states. Unit internals are derived from the stated unit, citing it; an unclear unit becomes wrong_unit, never a guess. Faithfully copied values stand. | ARCHITECTURE.md Components (Extraction agent); ISSUE_FORMAT.md 3.1 (S-UNIT) and 4.1 (note 3) | ARIADNE wins: no value conversion; microlitres stay a unit definition with scale -6. |
| C-17 | Citation check semantics and locators | The citation check confirms value containment (REQ-19, REQ-POL-03). Locators carry artifact hashes and optional byte offsets; 'first match' is called inadequate for ambiguous selectors. | The xpath resolves, the quote is verbatim in that node, and the value equals the quote under one fixed normaliser: equality, not containment. Locators are {xpath, quote}. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8.2 and 4 (source_locators), which refine ARCHITECTURE.md Components (precedence: register section 3) | Issue format wins. 'An xpath must select exactly one node': Issue format 2, Locator; 8.2 check 1 (this register, section 11, item 2). Byte offsets are a Stage 2 proposal; artifact hashes in locators are Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |
| C-18 | Not-mapped report linked to issues, coverage-checked | Every not-mapped entry must name at least one issue id, and every source token must be mapped or listed, which a coverage check enforces. | Q2 item 4 requires a not-mapped report of information EnzymeML cannot hold. Issue format v0.6 defines the not-mapped entry and its writer, the extraction agent (7.1 step 1; 8.1), with no mandatory issue link, and a value its field cannot hold reaches the field's missing issue through such an entry (8.1). The deterministic verifier does not search the input. | OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q2 (item 4); ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8, 8.1 and 11 | No mandatory issue link and no coverage check (C-01). The rest of the entry shape and who writes entries: Issue format 7.1 step 1; 8.1 (this register, section 11, item 3). |
| C-19 | Relationship graph for parameter links | The draft bundle carries a relationship graph linking parameters, substrates and datasets, with unsupported links kept in the not-mapped report. | Each parameter's evidence entry names its species_id and measurement_id, with a citation, which keeps parameter-substrate-dataset links traceable. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8.1 (Parameter links); OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q2 (item 4) | Issue format wins: Stage 1 uses the parameter-link evidence entries. The full relationship graph is a Stage 2 proposal. |
| C-20 | Approval record and approver | Approval has 19 required fields with authentication and hash bindings, checked against exact bytes; any authenticated scientist may approve; T-16 revokes approval after post-approval failures. | Approval needs the five conditions of ISSUE_FORMAT 7.2, including one named approver listed among the creators. The evidence file records {approved_by, approved_at}. The 19-field approval record is 'Stage 2, if ever'. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 7.2 and 11 | ISSUE_FORMAT 7.2 governs, including the creator condition. The package rule that any later change to a record voids its approval is in Issue format 7.2, Later changes (this register, section 11, item 8). Approved-file checksums are kept in the run log only (D13 A; Run records 2.9). The 19-field record, hash binding and authentication are Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11); revocation (T-16) is a Stage 2 proposal. |
| C-21 | Hashes, revisions, receipts and attestations everywhere | Ten hash domains (H0-H9), revision chains with compare-and-swap, receipts and idempotency, disposition attestations and provenance manifests are mandatory for every run. | Revision chains, file hashes, idempotency keys, the 19-field approval and disposition attestations are 'Stage 2, if ever'. Stage 1 inputs are stored unchanged with download date and checksum. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 11; OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q1 (item 1) | The full machinery is Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11); Stage 1 keeps the input checksum (Q1). Further software-computed checksums (prompts, configuration, packets, outputs, containment binding, approved files) are kept in the run log only (D13 A; Run records 2.9). D13 A adds none to the record; Issue format 11 states it (this register, section 11, item 7). |
| C-22 | Hashes required in agent output | The extraction agent's draft bundle must carry artifact SHA-256s, value digests on evidence and field bindings, and artifact hashes in locators. | Evidence entries carry no digests, SOFTWARE fields come from a separate assembly step, and software writes every id. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 8.1 and 3.1 | ARIADNE wins: no agent output carries a hash. Software computes any hash that is kept; which ones: D13 A, in the run log only (Run records 2.9). |
| C-23 | No workaround for the /version pattern defect | STD-BLOCK-001 forbids any pin change, schema repair, workaround or waiver, so admission denies every run and nothing can be approved. It recurs in 94 places in 23 files (section 8). | /version is validated against ^(\d+)\.\d+$ as an ECMA-262 whole-string match: one documented deviation, removed with a re-pin when upstream issue #35 is fixed. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 9 (item 1); [Workflow and review design consolidation](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated) (documentation record); [Issue format, changes from version 0.5](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#changes-from-version-05) | ARIADNE wins and supersedes every such statement, listed in section 8. Schema bytes stay unchanged. A schema-hash precondition with a check id and a suspension rule is a design rule, not part of Issue format (Workflow 4.1 step 3 and 4.4.1; Run records 2.9; this register, section 11, item 6, rejected). |
| C-24 | Rate-law species_id treated as a reference | Every present id and reference must resolve to a real id; a missing referenced id is a hard failure. | In a rate law, species_id holds the rate label v_<reaction id>; the deterministic verifier checks only that it is unique and does not treat it as a link to a species. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 9 (item 3) and 8 (C-REF) | ARIADNE wins: the documented reading in ISSUE_FORMAT 9 item 3 applies. The package's design text and specifications never mention rate laws; the conflict follows from its general reference rule. |
| C-25 | Data-owner admission for every run | Admission needs a data-owner assertion for the source and the model route, and the containment evidence manifest's authority must be the data owner. | Stage 1 uses public STRENDA DB entries. Data-owner conditions for processing, access and model endpoints are open question Q6, a Stage 2 question. Data owners approve any exchange. | OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q1 and Q6; README.md ledger: 2026-10-12 - Stage 1 benchmark datasets decided; ARCHITECTURE.md Proposed flow | Data-owner admission is Stage 2, tied to Q6. Stage 1 stores each input with download date and checksum (Q1); the proposed input-set manifest adds STRENDA ID, DOI and terms of use. The model route (one route, D11 A1) is recorded in design/RUNTIME.md. |
| C-26 | Containment proof unavailable, so every run denied | The containment proof is marked unavailable, so admission denies all runs; each of 18 mandatory paths must be routed through software (6 paths) or denied outright (12 paths). | Documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated): Hermes is the Stage 1 runtime, each agent attempt runs in a fresh process in which the agent is offered only ARIADNE's tools, and no run happens before the containment test suite passes. Decision of 2026-10-19 (section 4.3, Runtime): containment is proven in Stage 1 by an automated test suite. | Documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated); ARCHITECTURE.md Principles (Controlled); section 4.3 (Runtime); design/CONTAINMENT.md | Proposed: one test per path; for the 6 routed paths, the test tries the direct route. The nine paths found at the chosen Hermes tag are added as CT-19 to CT-27 (Containment 5). The suite covers prompt injection through STRENDA text ([Containment, section 7](CONTAINMENT.md#7-prompt-injection-tests-pi-01-to-pi-13)). No model call runs without a passing containment suite, and a failing path stops runs. Isolation: one container per attempt (D10 A). The first VERIFIED report at each Hermes pin, and any VERIFIED report after a FAILED one, gets a ledger Result entry (D24 A). |
| C-27 | Hermes pinned to an untagged main commit | Hermes is pinned to 82ef40ab, a main-branch commit of 2026-10-12 with no release tag, hard-coded across the specifications and cited by references R18-R26. | Documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated): Stage 1 pins a tagged Hermes release. | Documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated); section 3 (Pins); Runtime 3 | Proposed: re-pin to the tag chosen under D5 A and re-check there every Hermes interface the design relies on. R26's line numbers, some of which move at the new tag, and its mislabelled legacy-role anchor (section 9) are re-checked at that tag. The tag is recorded in Runtime 3. |
| C-28 | Per-attempt tool binding inside one process | The plugin's register(ctx) is written to bind an immutable capability for each attempt, with separate agent instances per attempt inside one long-running composition. | In the package's cached copy of Hermes hermes_cli/plugins.py (commit 82ef40ab), plugins are discovered once per process and tools register into a process-global registry. Documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated): one fresh process per attempt, offering only ARIADNE's tools; under D6 A there are none. | Documented Decision [Hermes Agent chosen as Stage 1 runtime](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated); the Hermes fact is re-checked at the D5 A tag (Runtime 4.1) | Proposed: one operating-system process per agent attempt; it reads its binding at start and exits after one attempt, which a test confirms. The process runs in its own container (D10 A). |
| C-29 | Finite budgets, including a fixed output-token cap | Configuration must be finite and explicit: fixed per-request limits on input, generated and reasoning tokens (uncalibrated; the values are in the Stage 2 appendix, Run records 6), a fixed max_tokens equal to the generated-token limit, and a budget reservation before every dispatch. | Token-cap decision of 2026-10-19 (section 4.3) and D12 A: per-request token caps are disabled during development and set later from what is measured as sufficient. | Token-cap decision of 2026-10-19 (section 4.3) and D12 A; Run records 4 and 6; Runtime 12.3 | Under D12 A, 'disabled' means max_tokens at the route's documented maximum, passed explicitly (one route, D11 A1). Usage is always measured. Zero limits on delegation, recursion, fallback and helper requests stay as containment rules. Provisional runaway guards in development (D19 A); evaluation caps from measurements, frozen before any test-set run (D12 A). Package values are in the Stage 2 appendix, Run records 6. |
| C-30 | Evaluation excluded; no single-agent entry point | The design charter excludes benchmark construction and empirical model evaluation, and roles run only inside the full controller after admission. | Each agent is evaluated on its own dataset, alone and in sequence, zero-shot against few-shot on the same items. Examples come only from the development set; agents never see expected outputs. | README.md ledger: 2026-10-12 - Stage 1 evaluation design decided; OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q4 | Proposed: single-agent and in-sequence modes, packets, expected-output proposals and run records, specified in Evaluation interfaces, sections 2, 3.5, 3.6, 4, 10 and 11, under D9 A. The Q4 item 4 separation is enforced by separate folders and the manifest and prompt checks (Evaluation interfaces 3.3 and 6), and the containment suite tests it with its own canaries (Containment 9, ES-01 to ES-03); canaries in expected-side files are an optional detector, used only if Tillman Heisner adopts them (Evaluation interfaces 5.3; 9, item 9). |
| C-31 | Question format and what is asked | A question has a requested_answer (explicit_value, correction, resolution, optional_disposition). The inquiry agent offers cannot_answer and asks for optional dispositions. | Software writes question_id and round; answer_type is value or choose_one, with two standard closing options. missing_optional and issues routed to human review are never asked. Questions follow the plain-language rule. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 5.1, 5.2, 5.3 and 7.2 | ARIADNE wins: explicit_value becomes a value question, correction and resolution a choose_one question, and cannot_answer a not_available answer item; empty LIST fields are never asked (C-06). The package rule that zero questions never means approval is kept; approval follows only ISSUE_FORMAT 7.2. |
| C-32 | Source conflicts and semantic dismissals | resolve_conflict and dismiss_semantic answers carry a rationale and become effective through an append-only disposition attestation (REV-C07). | A conflict or wrong value closes with the scientist's choice or a correction; a keep decision is recorded on the kept value's evidence and stays closed on re-check. Implausible but faithful values are out of scope. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 4.1, 5.4, 7.1 and 8.1 (Keep decision) | ARIADNE wins. A semantic dismissal of a conflict or wrong value maps to a keep decision on a source_conflict or wrong_value question; dismissals for implausibility are out of Stage 1 scope (ISSUE_FORMAT 4.1 note 3). Disposition attestations are Stage 2, if ever (ISSUE_FORMAT 11). |
| C-33 | Outdated ARIADNE baseline and conflicting names | The package binds to ARIADNE a2b3c77, before v0.4, the curator and generation before approval. It uses svc.ledger for its artifact store and the word 'verifier' for two actors. | The pre-consolidation design introduced v0.4, the curator (role removed on 2026-10-22, C10) and generation before approval; ARIADNE citations now refer to the consolidated documentation (section 3). 'Ledger' means only the provenance ledger of project records. | CONTRIBUTING.md Provenance ledger; [Workflow and review design consolidation](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated) (documentation record); [Issue format, changes from version 0.5](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#changes-from-version-05); ARCHITECTURE.md Proposed flow; section 3 (Pins) | ARIADNE wins: the package's ARIADNE citations refer to the consolidated documentation. Names follow the name table (section 5): run log, verification agent, deterministic verifier. Package ids stay out of ARCHITECTURE.md. |
| C-34 | Package decisions and claims presented as authority | DEC-01..11 are presented as approved decisions; REQ-06 and REQ-19 are marked enforced by design contract; 353/353 checks and an 'independent' AI review closure are reported. | A Decision is confirmed by the relevant authority and recorded in the append-only ledger with evidence; a ledger entry does not turn a proposal into a decision. Interim authority covers only the Stage 1 design. | CONTRIBUTING.md Provenance ledger; README.md ledger: 2026-10-12 - Interim decision authority for Stage 1 | DEC-01..11 are recorded as package defaults, not ARIADNE decisions, and the checks as the package's self-checks (section 10). Adoption happens only through new ledger entries; D4 A sets which entries (section 4.3). |
| C-35 | Repair attempt versus no retry to extraction | After invalid model output, one fresh same-role repair run with schema-error feedback is allowed for every role, counted in attempt and repair budgets (package section 6.4). | The loop runs from the deterministic verifier to the scientist; there is still no retry back to the extraction agent. | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 7.1 | D14 A: one technical re-run (Roles 7.7; Workflow 4.4.2), only after unparsable, truncated or crashed output, carrying only that error, counted and reported; it is not the forbidden retry (Issue format 7.1; 11 (this register, section 11, item 7)). D25 A confirms the reading: output that is not JSON or fails its format check is unparsable, a turn without a normal text exit is crashed, the limit applies per agent step, and an attempt that was never forwarded or that the model gateway made void uses no re-run (Handoffs 2.2, 2.13; Workflow 4.4.2). D28 A extends this to every void attempt, a start-up self-check that fails after the turn and a containment_hit included. |
| C-36 | Unsolicited scientist corrections and the answer channel | A scientist may send an unsolicited issue-linked correction, even from blocked, approval-ready or approved states (T-08); answers carry broker-verified authentication (package section 7.2). | Every answer answers a question (question_id), and its items name only issues that question tags; there is no answer without a question. 'The question does not fit' goes to human review. Approval records {approved_by, approved_at}, plus entered_by and original_ref when another named person entered it (Issue format 6.3). | ISSUE_FORMAT.md 5.1, 5.2, 6.2 and 7.2; OPEN_QUESTIONS.md Q7 | No direct correction channel in Stage 1. In a choice question the scientist can answer 'does not fit'; with no open question, a named person, normally the scientist, makes a structural edit with its basis (ISSUE_FORMAT 6.2), which triggers a re-check. Answer-file checksums are kept in the run log only (D13 A; Run records 2.9). Issue-linked corrections and an authenticated channel are Stage 2 (Q7). |

## 8. Superseded by the validation profile

**Rule:** every package statement that denies admission, runs or approval because of the EnzymeML `/version` pattern, or that requires the unmodified version pattern, is superseded by [Issue format, section 9](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#9-validation-profile) item 1. That item validates `/version` with the pattern the specification intends and records the deviation until the upstream schema is fixed ([EnzymeML/enzymeml-specifications issue 35](https://github.com/EnzymeML/enzymeml-specifications/issues/35)).

Only the version-pattern part of each listed line is superseded. Other content on the same line keeps its disposition in section 6, for example the requirement text, all-path containment admission, or the schema-hash check (a design rule: Workflow 4.1 and 4.4.1; item 6 was not taken into Issue format). Some lines only mention the check and are listed for completeness. The rule also covers matching statements in the package's self-check folders and in references.bib, which the table does not list.

The table lists the 94 places found by a mechanical search of the package (search pattern and scope in section 12).
