## 9. Known package defects

Found while reviewing the package. None of them is carried into ARIADNE: where a design document covers the same topic, it is written without the defect.

- Transition T-15 stands for several different changes (a diagnostic revision and an authorised content change) and skips the answer and patch guards.
- The workflow figure shows transition T-14 only into the deterministic check, while the specification has nine resume edges (T-14-1 to T-14-9); the resume guard G-RESUME sends recovery from the standards denial to the deterministic check, although that denial happens at intake, before any draft exists. Transition T-11 is missing from the figure.
- There is no coherent recovery path for a gap that the verification agent suspects but software cannot classify (the package's mechanical candidate), or for the standards denial.
- The attempt limit of the inquiry agent (4) equals the limit on clarification rounds (4), so it cannot also cover the inquiry run that follows the last answer; INT-C04 itself notes only that repairs and failed attempts can use the attempts up before the fourth round.
- Requirements REQ-06 and REQ-19 are marked as enforced by design contract, although the design narrows REQ-06 and goes beyond REQ-19.
- The design charter's decisions DEC-01 to DEC-11 are mostly the generator's own recommended defaults.
- Ten relative links in the package's Markdown files do not resolve; four of them point to the generator's own folders. The specifications and the charter also cite absolute paths in the generator's workspace as sources.
- The bundled Hermes Agent source copy lacks its MIT licence notice, and 21 of its 79 files are download-error placeholders.
- Reference R26 labels lines 782-786 of the delegation module as hidden legacy role handling, but at the package commit those lines strip two model-hidden ACP fields and do not touch the role. The real locations at the pinned tag, where the anchor for child construction also moves, are in [Runtime](RUNTIME.md), facts H-V8 and H-V8.1.
- The package does not mention the Tool Search setting, which Hermes switches on by default and which shows the model bridge tools instead of the tools it is offered ([Runtime](RUNTIME.md), fact H-V3.7).
- The package gives each agent a fixed allowance of 4 retry requests and names only the retry setting, recovery cycles, fallback and library retries of Hermes; Hermes has several other layers that send model requests, and not all of them can be switched off, so the allowance has no basis in the code ([Runtime](RUNTIME.md), fact H-V5.5).
- The package relies on the agent's `cwd` argument for a neutral working folder, but Hermes does not use that argument to choose the folder it reads context from ([Runtime](RUNTIME.md), fact H-V5.11).
- The package does not cover the mixture-of-agents path, by which a user message that starts with a fixed marker starts extra model calls ([Runtime](RUNTIME.md), fact H-V5.13).
- Nine paths that exist at the pinned tag are missing from the package's 18 containment paths; ARIADNE adds them as CT-19 to CT-27 ([Runtime](RUNTIME.md), fact H-V9; [Containment, section 5](CONTAINMENT.md#5-new-rows-ct-19-to-ct-27)).
- The package passes run_budget_seconds (the smaller of 300 seconds and the remaining lease) as a time limit for each agent attempt and calls it a cooperative control; in the files read at the pinned tag it stops no turn, and when no tool runs it only shortens the timeout of a non-streaming call near its end ([Runtime](RUNTIME.md), fact H-V11.6).
- The version-pattern defect is understated: the pattern matches no string at all, so a quoted version would fail too.
- The main document and the package README were edited after the package's "final independent" review, and the package's review-status statements contradict each other.

## 10. What does not count as ARIADNE evidence

The package reports 353 passed document checks, 103 matching example expectations and several AI reviews. These were produced in the same AI run that wrote the design, so ARIADNE treats them as the package's self-checks only. They check documents, not running software.

ARIADNE's own checks for this adoption are the register check against the mechanical inventory (section 12), the ledger checker, and the review before each commit.

## 11. Pending issue-format changes

Non-normative list of design findings that were proposed as changes to [Issue format](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md). Decided on 2026-10-20 (decision D7): items 1 to 5, 7 to 10, 12 to 15, 17 to 20, 22, 23, 26 and 27 are part of [Issue format](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md) version 0.5; items 6, 11, 16, 21, 24 and 25 were rejected and stay rules of the design documents. Register rows cite the Issue format section that now holds each adopted item, or the design document that holds each rejected one. Each item keeps its wording as proposed, including the curator role, which was removed on 2026-10-22 (C10). Issue format version 0.5 holds the adopted wording, which differs for items 10, 13, 18, 19, 23, 26 and 27 (see its changes from version 0.4); Issue format version 0.6 replaced version 0.5 on 2026-10-22, version 0.7 replaced version 0.6 on 2026-10-27 ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), version 0.8 replaced version 0.7 on 2026-10-28 ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), version 0.9 replaced version 0.8 later that day ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), and version 0.10, which replaced version 0.9 after that ([ledger](../README.md#2026-10-29---workflow-and-review-design-consolidated)), governs. The heading is kept so that existing links stay valid.

1. A schema "required" error at an empty field that has a field role (Issue format 3) is raised only as a missing_* issue; at an empty SOFTWARE field it stays wrong_type and goes to the curator. Accepted on 2026-10-19. **In version 0.5.**
2. The xpath in an evidence entry must select exactly one node. **In version 0.5.**
3. The shape of not-mapped entries and who writes them; no mandatory link to an issue. **In version 0.5.**
4. The number grammar used by the citation normaliser (Issue format 8.2). **In version 0.5.**
5. Software enforces which raiser may raise each issue kind ("Raised by" in Issue format 4.1), and technical failures are run-log records, not issues. **In version 0.5.**
6. A validation-profile precondition on the schema checksum, with a check id and a rule for suspending a run on mismatch. **Rejected on 2026-10-20; stays a design-document rule.**
7. The wording of Issue format sections 11 and 7.1, following decisions D13 and D14. **In version 0.5.**
8. Any later change to a record voids its approval. **In version 0.5.**

Proposed in phase P4 (found while writing Roles, Handoffs and Workflow):

9. Citations of reference fields: after S-ID a reference field (species_id, Complex.participants, vessel_id with several vessels, ids in parameter links) holds a software-written id that never equals its quote; the extraction agent's citation stays with the reference, marked interpreted; reference fields get citation checks 1 and 2 only, and the verification agent judges whether the reference is right. **In version 0.5.**
10. A unit the assembly step cannot derive is written as the stated spelling with no base units and an S-UNIT evidence entry marked not derived; the unit check raises wrong_unit for it, and the emptiness rule does not apply. **In version 0.5.**
11. Software writes answered_by, answered_at and approved_at when the answer file or approval statement is stored; answered_by is copied into every answer from the file. **Rejected on 2026-10-20; stays a design-document rule.**
12. Evidence for list fields is one entry per list element. **In version 0.5.**
13. The draft proposal carries the stated unit and the stated symbol as cited values; the assembly step never reads the input; S-SYMBOL copies a stated symbol only if it is unique in the record. **In version 0.5.**
14. The xpath dialect of the citation check is absolute XPath 1.0 location paths from the root element. **In version 0.5.**
15. An issue the verification agent must judge but leaves without a judgment (or with two verdicts) is recorded as not judged and routed as if uncertain; an added issue whose pointer resolves to nothing goes to the curator; both are recorded and counted. **In version 0.5.**
16. filled_by for objects that answer application creates is scientist (with the question id) for typed leaves and linking references, and software for their ids. **Rejected on 2026-10-20; stays a design-document rule.**
17. The one-node-per-xpath rule of item 2 also applies to source_locators in issues. **In version 0.5.**
18. Routing of issues raised in round 2 or later (no judgment) and of reopened issues: SOFTWARE-field issues to the curator, missing_optional to nobody, all others to the inquiry agent, including wrong_type, broken_reference or bad_citation on values the scientist or curator filled; a reopened issue keeps its round-1 verdict, if any, and is routed by it; a reopened issue without a verdict is routed like an issue raised in round 2 or later. Version 0.5 differs: a wrong_type, broken_reference or bad_citation issue on a value the curator wrote goes to the curator (Issue format 4.2, rule 6). **In version 0.5.**
19. Issues of kinds the deterministic verifier never raises keep their status on re-check and close only by an answer or curator action; its own kinds that it no longer finds close with closed_by recheck. Version 0.5 differs: the split is by raiser, and any open issue raised by the verification agent, a wrong_unit included, stays open on re-check (Issue format 7.1, On re-check). **In version 0.5.**
20. "The last re-check" of Issue format 7.2 condition 2 is a final check of the final files as a round of its own; any issue it raises or reopens goes to the curator as a sign of a software defect. **In version 0.5.**
21. Askable issues that no question tags stay open and are asked in the next round; a question round with no question, although its packet holds askable issues, fails its format check and gets the one technical re-run. **Rejected on 2026-10-20; stays a design-document rule.**
22. A record that ended as not approvable continues by a curator's structural edit that closes an unavailable issue (alternative: a new run of the entry). **In version 0.5.**
23. A curator item the curator cannot close may be sent to the inquiry agent and asked as a lab fact. **In version 0.5.**
24. Under version 0.4 alone, a wrong_type issue for a schema "required" error at an empty field that is neither extracted nor a SOFTWARE field goes to the curator; item 1 removes the case. **Rejected on 2026-10-20; stays a design-document rule.**
25. The curator may withdraw a run that stalls while waiting for people, as an end of its own, never approval and never a technical failure. **Rejected on 2026-10-20; stays a design-document rule.**
26. A stated value that its field cannot hold reaches the source_value of the field's missing issue through the extraction agent's not-mapped entry, and that entry reaches the final not-mapped report only if the field ends "not available". **In version 0.5.**
27. A wrong parameter link (parameter links live in the evidence file, not an EnzymeML field) is raised as wrong_value at the parameter's pointer, with the link's locators. **In version 0.5.**

## 12. Method and traceability

**How the inventory was made.** A script read the package files line by line, outside the package folder and without running any package code. It collected:
- every main-document heading;
- every top-level key of each specification file and every data format in the contract schema;
- the guard predicates, transitions, containment paths, explicit denials and operations;
- every identifier of the families below.

**How section 8 was made.** A second script searched the package for the terms STD-BLOCK, standards_preflight, standard_incompatib(ility), op.standards and full_pinned_schema_valid, and for the phrases "no validator override", "no relaxed (final) validity" and the quoted-version and quote-workaround wording. It read .md, .yaml, .json, .csv, .mmd and .txt files and skipped the folders validation/, review/, evidence/original_drafts/ and the Hermes source copy.

The register in section 6 is checked against the inventory: every item appears exactly once. The inventory, the hit list and both scripts are kept by Tillman Heisner outside the repository and can be re-run on request. Re-run on 2026-10-21 (phase P7): the inventory and the hit list are unchanged, and every item still appears once in section 6.

**What section 6 covers**

| Part | Count |
|---|---|
| Main-document headings | 42 |
| Package files and folders | 30 |
| Top-level keys of specification files | 175 |
| Data formats in the contract schema | 59 |
| Guard predicates | 41 |
| Guards (G-...) | 15 |
| Transition identifiers T-... (16 transitions, 24 specification entries, other mentions) | 25 |
| Operations and transformations | 19 |
| Containment paths | 18 |
| Explicit denials | 25 |
| Requirements REQ / REQ-POL | 19 / 13 |
| Invariants INV | 19 |
| Scenarios SC / POL-C / INT-C / REV-C | 35 / 24 / 4 / 22 |
| Charter decisions DEC | 11 |
| Design records CORE-ADR / ADR-POL / ADR-INT | 16 / 7 / 4 |
| Algorithms CORE-ALG and limit CORE-LIM | 3 / 1 |
| Hash domains H0-H9 | 10 |
| Algorithm steps I / V / X | 7 / 6 / 6 |

**Research basis.** The package cites ten papers. ARIADNE treats each as inspiration, not evidence: no performance, safety or scaling result transfers to ARIADNE, whose own checks are those of section 10. For each paper, what ARIADNE takes from it and where the transfer stops:

- R01: AutoGen, https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.08155. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: fixed roles and a person in the loop, with typed handoffs between steps instead of an open conversation between agents. Transfer limit: the paper says nothing about whether extraction is valid or whether agents are isolated.
- R02: Magentic-One, https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04468. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: progress tracking and recovery become the run log and resume, with steps scheduled by software and no supervising agent. Transfer limit: its results on orchestrating general tasks do not carry over.
- R03: Towards an AI co-scientist, https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18864v1. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: generating and checking are separate, under the scientist's direction. Rejected: agents ranking their own outputs, or a tournament, as a way to approve. Transfer limit: generating hypotheses is a different task from writing records that must rest on a source.
- R04: AgentDojo, https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13352. Inspiration, not evidence. Adopted as a principle: text from the source is untrusted, and what an agent can do is bounded from outside ([Containment, section 7](CONTAINMENT.md#7-prompt-injection-tests-pi-01-to-pi-13)). Transfer limit: no rate at which defences succeed carries over.
- R05: Revisiting multi-agent debate as test-time scaling, https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.22960. Inspiration, not evidence. Rejected: debate or agreement between agents as a test of truth, and extra agents by default. Transfer limit: its findings depend on the task.
- R06: Towards a science of scaling agent systems, https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.08296v1. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: every role must be justified, the cost of coordination is counted, and one step at a time works on a record. Transfer limit: no gain from scaling carries over.
- R07: XFlow, https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14790. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: typed formats between steps and state changes that only software makes ([Workflow](WORKFLOW.md)). Transfer limit: it does not validate this workflow.
- R08: Agentproof, https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.20356. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: the reachability review done by hand on the transition table ([Workflow, section 7](WORKFLOW.md#7-manual-reachability-review)). Transfer limit: a safe graph of states says nothing about whether the science is right.
- R09: EviSearch, https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14165. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: attribution by reading the source, and routing open points to people. Rejected: memory that changes as the agent learns (test CT-12 in [Containment, section 4.12](CONTAINMENT.md#412-ct-12---memory-skills-and-background-context)). Transfer limit: its clinical results do not carry over.
- R10: Evaluating and guarding citation faithfulness in agentic scientific synthesis, https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20527. Inspiration, not evidence. Adapted: checking against linked evidence, with model verdicts treated as fallible ([Issue format 8.2](../ISSUE_FORMAT.md#82-citation-check); the verification agent). Transfer limit: a citation that matches its source does not make a value true or a record complete.

**References.** EnzymeML specifications at commit bf9321f: https://github.com/EnzymeML/enzymeml-specifications/blob/bf9321f720eaff48d315bad52aebc1765cf42188/schemes/v2/enzymeml-v2.json. Upstream issue #35 on the version pattern: https://github.com/EnzymeML/enzymeml-specifications/issues/35. Hermes Agent at tag v2026.9.24, commit f97608f178d1ffeca59860195ab7da295f7c8e5f: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/tree/f97608f178d1ffeca59860195ab7da295f7c8e5f.
